# Scheduler clock reuse (#1)

The source change is `1609241456b22e174981792e1fa9b5f0c743a2c2` to `9f851500227bc39e08b95a5737325d93905e3e96`.
Both Nix packages use ReleaseSafe and the baseline CPU target.
The implementation commit preceded the after measurements.

## Clock calls

An owned loopback fixture traced three idle wakes and twenty sequential local UDP answers.
Health and cache were disabled. The synthetic localhost answer required no upstream exchange.
The fixture traced each binary from startup, not the production service.

| Observation | Before | Shared tick |
|---|---:|---:|
| Monotonic calls across three idle wakes | 9 | 3 |
| Monotonic calls across twenty UDP queries | 140 | 80 |
| Completion log lines for those queries | 20 | 20 |

The idle path now issues one monotonic call per wake instead of three.
The UDP fixture now issues four calls per query instead of seven.
Two calls measure query duration. Separate receive and send dispatches each acquire their scheduler timestamp.

All twenty logged durations match the fresh start/end difference, truncated to microseconds.
Substitution of the scheduler timestamp for either endpoint disagrees with every captured duration.
These counts do not explain the platform's syscall-versus-vDSO choice.

## Boundaries

Each dispatch acquires `tick_ns` after event retrieval.
The cache and hosts reload reuse it within that dispatch. The forward scheduler uses the same snapshot.
Time inside the tick consumes absolute timeout budgets.
Query-duration endpoints retain fresh samples.
Darwin relative timers and teardown retain independent clock reads.

The throughput fixture uses 4096 warm names with thirty-two outstanding queries.
It owns its listener and upstream sockets. It does not query production or public upstreams.
Three rotating three-second trials cover each transport in each matrix.
Resolver CPU 2, generator CPUs 0/3, and upstream CPU 1 retain the previous comparison setup.
Sequential untimed warmup permits one outstanding UDP query. Each exchange has a one-second deadline without retries.

The comparison excludes TLS and hosts. Completion logs go to `/dev/null`, without stderr backpressure.
It makes no host-isolation or fixed-frequency claim.
An initial slowdown affected both variants. The unchanged matrix was repeated, with host counters sampled once per second.
The capture retains both matrices and all low trials. No cause is assigned to the initial slowdown.

## Correctness

Linux forward tests passed 63 cases with one Darwin skip. Logging passed 14 cases and health passed 21.
The focused clock selection passed five cases with one Darwin skip. Counts include root-import tests.
The interruption and hosts-reload selections each passed three cases.
The portable checks passed:

- Config: 16 tests.
- Wire: 42 tests.
- Resolver: 62 tests.
- Foundation: four tests.
- Benchmark smoke.
- Formatting and both ziglint commands.

Both x86_64-linux and aarch64-macos passed ten semantic build steps. These are not native foreign runs.

Three native controls failed at their intended assertions, then passed after restoration.
A fresh scheduler read mixed clock domains and skipped the synthetic due probe.
An omitted refresh retained zero. A cached completion timestamp reported the synthetic sixty-second duration.
The Darwin timer regression received semantic compilation only, without a native or mutation result.
Cache and hosts tests do not isolate each seconds call site. Admission clock calls remain unchanged.

Failed fixture preparation remains in the capture and does not count as regression evidence.
The first shared-clock control used a private method and failed compilation.
The first completion fixture expected admission for an unsupported first member and dereferenced null.
The corrected fixture models a terminal local failure without an upstream operation. It does not prove real client delivery.

## Throughput

The table pools both complete matrices, with six trials per cell and no discarded rows.
Values are medians. The source change is the only package variable.

| Health / members | Transport | Before QPS | Shared tick QPS | Change | Before CPU µs/query | Shared tick CPU µs/query |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 2 / 1 | UDP | 186090.1 | 199027.8 | +6.95% | 4.276 | 3.943 |
| 2 / 1 | TCP | 138427.0 | 148118.0 | +7.00% | 5.295 | 4.875 |
| 0 / 1 | UDP | 182467.6 | 189336.5 | +3.76% | 4.265 | 3.941 |
| 0 / 1 | TCP | 135248.5 | 143438.4 | +6.06% | 5.310 | 4.869 |
| 2 / 16 | UDP | 180897.3 | 195896.0 | +8.29% | 4.387 | 4.053 |
| 2 / 16 | TCP | 135303.9 | 146752.5 | +8.46% | 5.463 | 5.047 |

The health-enabled, one-member geometric mean improves 6.98%.
The two matrices completed 32,446,201 queries across 72 timed trials.
Every trial reported zero loss and only NOERROR. No timed trial grew the upstream log.
Primary median RSS was 60604 KiB for both binaries. This is not an allocation bound.

The initial health-disabled TCP median changed -30.76%. The repeat changed +6.20%.
The initial matrix includes a 42807.8 QPS primary TCP trial and health-disabled UDP trials as low as 12714.8 QPS.
The repeat does not reproduce those severe drops. All six pooled medians improve.
Short shared-host trials do not establish saturation capacity or production latency. The observed gain is not a guarantee.

The repeat host sample records CPU pressure and zero steal ticks across its 115-second interval.
Those later counters do not diagnose the initial slowdown. No host process was stopped for measurement.

The separate 199 Hz profile contains 579 samples with zero lost samples. Its extra timed rows do not enter the comparison.
Disassembly of `Driver.drive` removes both direct clock syscall sites and their indirect clock entry paths.
The first disassembly request targeted an absent `Runtime.step` symbol. The corrected capture targets the emitted `Driver.drive` symbol.
Clock measurements remain present in other functions by design. No vDSO redesign accompanies this change.

The change remains retained. The primary clock-count target passes, and the repeat finds no throughput regression above five percent.
Both matrices remain visible. No production tracing, restart, or deployment occurred.

## Raw artifacts

The headers identify the original files and their SHA-256 hashes.
Trailing whitespace is removed. Separator prefixes replace lines that resemble conflict markers.
The clock helper and comparison helper run from the source root inside `nix develop`.
The comparison helper imports tracked `scripts/compare-coredns.py` from the stated source revision.

--- ARTIFACT .lab/clock/config.md sha256=f80699aeebb0042fa5213351563250bea7bc5349393da8b57eef8c1fb87d3d5a ---
# Tick clock reuse, 2026-09-08

The owner requested one shared monotonic scheduler timestamp per event tick.
This is one bounded source change, not a continuation of the completed optimization campaign.

The primary check counts monotonic syscalls in an owned strace fixture.
The baseline is main@1609241456b22e174981792e1fa9b5f0c743a2c2.
Three idle waits issue nine monotonic clock calls. Twenty local UDP answers issue 140 calls.
The target is one scheduler sample per dispatch, plus fresh query-duration measurements.
Darwin relative timer conversion and teardown keep independent clock samples.

The secondary comparison uses the existing runtime helper, including its sequential untimed warmup.
Both Nix packages use ReleaseSafe and baseline CPU settings.
Three rotating three-second trials cover UDP32 and TCP32 over 4096 warm names.
Health/member cases are 2/1, 0/1, and 2/16.
Resolver CPU 2, generator CPUs 0/3, and owned upstream CPU 1 remain unchanged.
A throughput regression above five percent requires investigation.
No gain threshold governs this owner-requested reduction in redundant clock work.
All timed rows require zero loss, NOERROR, and no upstream traffic.
The fixture excludes production, public upstreams, TLS, hosts, and stderr backpressure.

Native tests and existing deadline semantics are correctness gates.
Fresh duration samples prevent zero durations from a frozen scheduler clock.
Time inside a tick consumes absolute timeout budgets. No cached relative timeout can renew them.
Two read-only reviewers inspect both backends. Foreign compilation is not native execution.
Verified commits are pushed by default. Production deployment remains separate.
--- END ARTIFACT .lab/clock/config.md ---

--- ARTIFACT .lab/clock/capture.py sha256=ce3fde8191305faa8486e8014fe71250b811a7371b1edeac9670f21e866ec11a ---
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import signal
import socket
import struct
import subprocess
import sys
import time

binary = str(Path(sys.argv[1]).resolve())
root = Path(sys.argv[2]).resolve()
root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=False)
with socket.socket() as reservation:
    reservation.bind(("127.0.0.1", 0))
    port = reservation.getsockname()[1]
config = root / "config.zon"
config.write_text('.{ .listen = .{"127.0.0.1:%d"}, .zones = .{.{ .suffix = ".", .cache = null, .upstreams = .{.{ .address = "127.0.0.1:1" }}, .max_fails = 0 }} }' % port)
trace = root / "trace.txt"


def query(identifier):
    packet = struct.pack("!6H", identifier, 0x100, 1, 0, 0, 0) + b"\x09localhost\x00\x00\x01\x00\x01"
    with socket.socket(socket.AF_INET, socket.SOCK_DGRAM) as client:
        client.settimeout(0.2)
        client.connect(("127.0.0.1", port))
        client.send(packet)
        response = client.recv(512)
    assert struct.unpack("!6H", response[:12]) == (identifier, 0x8500, 1, 1, 0, 0)
    assert response[12:len(packet)] == packet[12:]
    assert response[-4:] == b"\x7f\x00\x00\x01"


with (root / "resolver.log").open("w") as log:
    tracer = subprocess.Popen(["strace", "-tt", "-e", "trace=clock_gettime,io_uring_enter,write,writev", "-o", str(trace), binary, "-c", str(config)], stdout=log, stderr=log, start_new_session=True)
    try:
        for identifier in range(100):
            assert tracer.poll() is None
            try:
                query(identifier)
                break
            except (TimeoutError, ConnectionRefusedError):
                time.sleep(0.02)
        else:
            raise AssertionError("No DNS readiness")
        time.sleep(0.1)
        start = len(trace.read_bytes())
        time.sleep(3.2)
        idle = trace.read_bytes()[start:].decode()
        (root / "idle.txt").write_text(idle)
        start = len(trace.read_bytes())
        for identifier in range(1000, 1020):
            query(identifier)
        time.sleep(0.1)
        queries = trace.read_bytes()[start:].decode()
        (root / "queries.txt").write_text(queries)
        result = {
            "binary": binary,
            "idle_monotonic_calls": idle.count("clock_gettime(CLOCK_MONOTONIC"),
            "idle_waits": idle.count("IORING_ENTER_GETEVENTS"),
            "udp_queries": 20,
            "query_monotonic_calls": queries.count("clock_gettime(CLOCK_MONOTONIC"),
            "query_log_lines": queries.count("writev(2,"),
        }
        assert result["query_log_lines"] == 20
        assert tracer.poll() is None
        print(json.dumps(result, indent=2))
        (root / "result.json").write_text(json.dumps(result, indent=2) + "\n")
    finally:
        if tracer.poll() is None:
            os.killpg(tracer.pid, signal.SIGTERM)
        tracer.wait(timeout=5)
--- END ARTIFACT .lab/clock/capture.py ---

--- ARTIFACT .lab/clock/check-durations.py sha256=2508a511aa81263c8b6c85ff9b32927d02a38edc8a4a647b343f7ea4f8522032 ---
# SPEC sections 1 and 4: fresh duration endpoints remain independent of scheduler time.
import json
from pathlib import Path
import re

root = Path('.lab/clock/after-trace')
clock = re.compile(r'clock_gettime\(CLOCK_MONOTONIC, \{tv_sec=(\d+), tv_nsec=(\d+)\}')
readings = []
triples = []
for line in (root / 'queries.txt').read_text().splitlines():
    found = clock.search(line)
    if found:
        readings.append(int(found[1]) * 1_000_000_000 + int(found[2]))
    if 'writev(2,' in line:
        triples.append(readings[-3:])
logs = [line for line in (root / 'resolver.log').read_text().splitlines() if 'event=query ' in line][-20:]
assert len(triples) == len(logs) == 20
assert len(readings) == 80
rows = []
for (tick, started, finished), line in zip(triples, logs):
    match = re.search(r'duration_ms=(\d+)\.(\d{3})\b', line)
    assert match is not None
    reported_us = int(match[1]) * 1000 + int(match[2])
    assert tick < started < finished
    assert reported_us == (finished - started) // 1000
    # Both wrong endpoint substitutions disagree with every observed completion line.
    assert reported_us != (finished - tick) // 1000
    assert reported_us != max(0, tick - started) // 1000
    rows.append({'tick_ns': tick, 'started_ns': started, 'finished_ns': finished, 'reported_us': reported_us})
(root / 'durations.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows, indent=2) + '\n')
print('20 completion durations match fresh endpoints, not the scheduler timestamp')
--- END ARTIFACT .lab/clock/check-durations.py ---

--- ARTIFACT .lab/clock/repeat.py sha256=80ff861975cd745c6932f182048d6ae4d0f1ad88431863b5f4645f759c417aec ---
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import signal
import subprocess
import threading
import time

stopped = threading.Event()


def sample():
    with Path('.lab/clock/repeat-host.jsonl').open('w') as output:
        for _ in range(360):
            row = {'unix_ns': time.time_ns(), 'load': os.getloadavg()}
            for name, path in [('stat', '/proc/stat'), ('cpu_pressure', '/proc/pressure/cpu'), ('memory_pressure', '/proc/pressure/memory')]:
                row[name] = Path(path).read_text()
            output.write(json.dumps(row) + '\n')
            output.flush()
            if stopped.wait(1):
                break


command = [
    'python3', '.lab/runtime/compare.py',
    '--variant', 'before=/tmp/z53-clock-before/bin/z53',
    '--variant', 'tick=/tmp/z53-clock-after/bin/z53',
    '--output', '.lab/clock/comparison-repeat', '--trials', '3', '--duration', '3',
]
monitor = threading.Thread(target=sample)
monitor.start()
process = None
try:
    process = subprocess.Popen(command, start_new_session=True)
    status = process.wait(timeout=300)
    if status:
        raise subprocess.CalledProcessError(status, command)
finally:
    if process is not None and process.poll() is None:
        os.killpg(process.pid, signal.SIGTERM)
        try:
            process.wait(timeout=5)
        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
            os.killpg(process.pid, signal.SIGKILL)
            process.wait(timeout=5)
    stopped.set()
    monitor.join()
--- END ARTIFACT .lab/clock/repeat.py ---

--- ARTIFACT .lab/clock/review-resolution.txt sha256=a79800075b1ea27538e52bb2aa86cbf12390e08e21c1708569389455d9d89c2c ---
Parent review resolution

Both reviewers found no production deadline-renewal defect.
The Darwin reviewer requested regression coverage for fresh clock exceptions.
A Linux completion test now rejects the cached-timestamp mutation.
A Darwin timer test now observes a bounded, isolated queue.
Darwin received semantic compilation only. Its native result and mutation result remain unproved.
Admission clock calls remain unchanged. The source and forthcoming trace provide separate evidence.
No injected clock framework or pure subtraction-only seam was added.

The specification now distinguishes event retrieval from an actual wait.
The Linux drain guard now explains why teardown does not acquire a scheduler sample.
The decisions file records the lack of isolated tests for every cache-facing seconds call site.

The proposed default tick_ns = 0 does not initialize allocator.create(Runtime) storage.
The explicit init assignments therefore remain required. No misleading default was added.
Division by ns_per_s stays explicit. A two-backend helper does not remove meaningful complexity here.
The runtime.now() facade remains necessary at tests/runtime_forward.zig:778,1012,1352,3673.

The shared-clock mutation changes Driver.drive's initial timestamp, not merely its probe loop.
With the synthetic future tick, mixed timestamp domains expire the client and skip the due probe.
The failure is expected false, found true at the absent probe.
The refresh mutation retains zero and fails expected false, found true.
The completion mutation reports the synthetic 60-second duration and fails expected true, found false.

Failed preparation is retained, not counted as a regression result.
The first shared mutation failed compilation after a test called private probesActive.
The corrected public-field assertion passed before the valid mutation run.
The first completion fixture selected an unsupported first member and got null admission.
The corrected Linux fixture publishes a modeled terminal local failure without an upstream operation.
It does not assert a real client delivery or new admission sample.
--- END ARTIFACT .lab/clock/review-resolution.txt ---

--- ARTIFACT .lab/runtime/compare.py sha256=377176246030f4acb38e0b9765fabd76e773d9bac35333f91a06491a9914519b ---
import argparse
import fcntl
import hashlib
import importlib.util
import json
import math
import os
from pathlib import Path
import random
import shutil
import statistics
import subprocess
import time
from warm import warm as warm_queries

spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('bench', 'scripts/compare-coredns.py')
bench = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
spec.loader.exec_module(bench)
parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
parser.add_argument('--variant', action='append', required=True, help='label=binary')
parser.add_argument('--output', type=Path, required=True)
parser.add_argument('--trials', type=int, default=3)
parser.add_argument('--duration', type=int, default=3)
parser.add_argument('--cases', default='2:1,0:1,2:16')
parser.add_argument('--profile', action='store_true')
args = parser.parse_args()
assert 1 <= args.trials <= 5 and 1 <= args.duration <= 10
cases = [tuple(map(int, case.split(':'))) for case in args.cases.split(',')]
assert all(health in (0, 2) and 1 <= members <= 16 for health, members in cases)
lock = open('.lab/measurement.lock', 'w')
fcntl.flock(lock, fcntl.LOCK_EX)
root = args.output
root.mkdir()
variants = [(label, str(Path(binary).resolve())) for label, binary in (v.split('=', 1) for v in args.variant)]
metadata = dict(source=bench.command(['git', 'rev-parse', 'HEAD']).strip(), optimization='ReleaseSafe',
                target='baseline unless explicitly labeled', cases=cases, trials=args.trials,
                duration_s=args.duration, names=4096, resolver_cpu=2, generator_cpus=[0,3], upstream_cpu=1,
                logging='/dev/null', GOMAXPROCS=1, uname=list(os.uname()),
                warmup='sequential UDP, one outstanding, one-second deadline, no retries',
                binaries={label:dict(path=binary, sha256=hashlib.sha256(Path(binary).read_bytes()).hexdigest())
                          for label,binary in variants})
(root/'metadata.json').write_text(json.dumps(metadata, indent=2)+'\n')
names = [f'q{index:05}.bench.test. A\n' for index in range(4096)]
random.Random(53).shuffle(names)
data = root/'queries.txt'
data.write_text(''.join(names))
upstream = bench.port()
upstream_log = root/'upstream.log'
config = root/'upstream.Corefile'
config.write_text(f'''bench.test:{upstream} {{
 bind 127.0.0.1
 log
 template IN A {{
  answer "{{{{ .Name }}}} 3600 IN A 192.0.2.1"
 }}
}}
''')
rows=[]
with bench.server(shutil.which('coredns'), config, upstream, upstream_log, 1):
    for health,members in cases:
        for trial in range(args.trials):
            start = trial % len(variants)
            for label,binary in variants[start:]+variants[:start]:
                number = bench.port()
                prefix = f'h{health}-m{members}-t{trial}-{label}'
                config = root/(prefix+'.conf')
                if Path(binary).name == 'coredns':
                    peers = ' '.join([f'127.0.0.1:{upstream}']*members)
                    config.write_text(f'.:{number} {{\n bind 127.0.0.1\n log\n errors\n cache 3600\n forward . {peers} {{\n policy sequential\n max_fails {health}\n }}\n}}\n')
                else:
                    peers = ','.join([f'.{{ .address = "127.0.0.1:{upstream}" }}']*members)
                    config.write_text(f'.{{ .listen = .{{ "127.0.0.1:{number}" }}, .zones = .{{ .{{ .suffix = ".", .upstreams = .{{ {peers} }}, .max_fails = {health} }} }} }}')
                with bench.server(binary, config, number, os.devnull, 2) as process:
                    try:
                        warm = warm_queries(root,data,number,prefix+'-warm')
                    except (subprocess.TimeoutExpired, TimeoutError) as error:
                        detail = dict(command=getattr(error,'cmd',None), timeout_s=getattr(error,'timeout',1),
                                      resolver_pid=process.pid, resolver_status=process.poll())
                        (root/(prefix+'-warm-timeout.json')).write_text(json.dumps(detail,indent=2)+'\n')
                        for stream,value in [('stdout',getattr(error,'stdout',None)),('stderr',getattr(error,'stderr',None))]:
                            if value is not None:
                                text=value.decode(errors='replace') if isinstance(value,bytes) else value
                                (root/(prefix+'-warm-'+stream+'.txt')).write_text(text)
                        raise
                    assert warm['sent']==4096 and warm['completed']==4096 and warm['lost']==0
                    upstream_bytes = upstream_log.stat().st_size
                    for protocol in ['udp','tcp']:
                        case = prefix+'-'+protocol
                        recorder = None
                        if args.profile and protocol=='udp':
                            command = ['perf','record','-F','199','-e','cycles:u','--call-graph','dwarf',
                                       '-p',str(process.pid),'-o',str(root/(case+'.data')),'--','sleep',str(args.duration+1)]
                            recorder = subprocess.Popen(command, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT, text=True)
                            time.sleep(.1)
                        before = bench.cpu_seconds(process.pid)
                        try:
                            result = bench.measure(root,data,number,protocol,32,case,args.duration)
                        finally:
                            if recorder is not None:
                                output,_ = recorder.communicate(timeout=args.duration+3)
                                (root/(case+'-record.txt')).write_text(' '.join(command)+'\n'+output)
                                assert recorder.returncode==0,output
                        result.update(variant=label,health=health,members=members,trial=trial,protocol=protocol,
                                      cpu_s=bench.cpu_seconds(process.pid)-before,rss_kib=bench.rss_kib(process.pid),
                                      upstream_bytes=upstream_log.stat().st_size-upstream_bytes)
                        assert result['sent']==result['completed'] and result['lost']==0
                        assert result['upstream_bytes']==0
                        rows.append(result)
                        (root/'results.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,indent=2)+'\n')
                        print(json.dumps(result),flush=True)
                        if recorder is not None:
                            report=bench.command(['perf','report','--stdio','--no-children','--call-graph','none','--percent-limit','.5','-i',str(root/(case+'.data'))])
                            (root/(case+'-report.txt')).write_text(report)
summary=[]
for health,members in cases:
    for label,_ in variants:
        qps=[]
        for protocol in ['udp','tcp']:
            group=[row for row in rows if row['health']==health and row['members']==members and row['variant']==label and row['protocol']==protocol]
            values=[row['qps'] for row in group]
            qps.append(statistics.median(values))
            summary.append(dict(health=health,members=members,variant=label,protocol=protocol,median=qps[-1],minimum=min(values),maximum=max(values),
                                cpu_us=statistics.median(row['cpu_s']/row['completed']*1e6 for row in group)))
        print(label,health,members,'primary',math.sqrt(math.prod(qps)))
(root/'summary.json').write_text(json.dumps(summary,indent=2)+'\n')
--- END ARTIFACT .lab/runtime/compare.py ---

--- ARTIFACT .lab/runtime/warm.py sha256=4a3f031a0e86170e5f40754707031894808375614160a8c82c0c41fd34964733 ---
"""Untimed warmup with one UDP request outstanding and no retry."""
import json
import socket
import struct
import time


def warm(root, data, number, label):
    lines=data.read_text().splitlines()
    assert len(lines)==4096
    target=('127.0.0.1',number)
    started=time.monotonic_ns()
    duration_ns_max=0
    with socket.socket(socket.AF_INET,socket.SOCK_DGRAM) as client:
        client.settimeout(1)
        for identifier,line in enumerate(lines):
            name,kind=line.split()
            assert kind=='A' and name.endswith('.bench.test.')
            labels=name.rstrip('.').split('.')
            question=b''.join(bytes([len(label)])+label.encode('ascii') for label in labels)
            question+=b'\x00'+struct.pack('!HH',1,1)
            request=struct.pack('!HHHHHH',identifier,0x0100,1,0,0,0)+question
            before=time.monotonic_ns()
            assert client.sendto(request,target)==len(request)
            try:
                response,peer=client.recvfrom(65535)
            except TimeoutError:
                (root/(label+'-timeout.json')).write_text(json.dumps(dict(identifier=identifier,name=name,completed=identifier),indent=2)+'\n')
                raise
            duration_ns_max=max(duration_ns_max,time.monotonic_ns()-before)
            assert peer==target and len(response)>=12
            returned,flags,questions,answers,authority,additional=struct.unpack('!HHHHHH',response[:12])
            assert returned==identifier and flags&0x820f==0x8000
            assert questions==1 and answers==1 and authority==0 and additional==0
            assert response[12:12+len(question)]==question
    result=dict(sent=len(lines),completed=len(lines),lost=0,rcode='NOERROR',
                elapsed_ns=time.monotonic_ns()-started,duration_ns_max=duration_ns_max,
                method='sequential UDP, one outstanding, one-second deadline, no retries')
    (root/(label+'.json')).write_text(json.dumps(result,indent=2)+'\n')
    return result
--- END ARTIFACT .lab/runtime/warm.py ---
